- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 4
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 8
        - Boldness: 7
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 6
        - WarmongerHate: 3

        - GuardedBias: 7

        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 9
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 10
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 4
        - MinorCivWarBias: 1
        - DenounceWillingness: 7
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 8
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (INCREASE Shoshone urgency - Moson Kahni produced Atomic Bomb this turn. Boldness increased to 7 (from 6) - more aggressive response needed to nuclear threat. HostileBias 6 (from 5) - Shoshone escalations require stronger diplomatic signaling. DiplomaticBalance reduced to 5 (from 6) - priority shifting toward military solutions over negotiation. Shoshone now has both cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced) AND nuclear capability. Must accelerate conquest or face nuclear retaliation. Naval battles with Shoshone (lost 2 Battleships) show their naval strength must be countered.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Electronics
        - Rationale: Computers already researched (completed this turn), Electronics is next unlocked. Electronics allows Police Station construction, improving city security levels against enemy spies and espionage. Critical protection for high-value science cities (Paris: 346 science/turn) and production centers. Also unlocks path toward Advanced Ballistics (more nuclear weapons) and alternative energy technologies. Security essential as Shoshone (355 tourism) and rivals likely stepping up espionage. Police Stations also provide culture synergy with Iron Fist tenet.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Ottoman relationship to neutral (0/0 from -50/-50). Ottomans eliminated with Istanbul captured - no longer a relevant diplomatic entity. No hostility modifiers needed as they pose no threat with only 1 remaining city (Bursa) and their civilization effectively destroyed. Maintaining any negative modifiers is unnecessary and could potentially affect diplomatic calculations with other civs who may have relationships with the Ottomans. This signals our peaceful stance toward the remaining Ottoman territory while focusing on consolidation and cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 156
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Brazil relationship to neutral (0/0 from 15/15). Brazil is capitulated Shoshone vassal and irrelevant to World Congress (only 6 votes). Resources better focused on Poland, Siam, World Congress diplomacy, and domestic production. Brazil poses no threat (1515 military vs France 7017) and won't impact primary cultural threat from Shoshone or Decolonization vote. Maintaining positive modifiers wastes diplomatic capacity on a defeated opponent.
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Edinburgh
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Edinburgh (city-state ally of Shoshone) declared war on France this turn due to Sphere of Influence over Palatium. Set relationship to highly hostile (-50/-50). Edinburgh only provides 1 vote to Shoshone, so diplomatic penalty is minimal. However, this sends a clear message about consequences of attacking France. Edinburgh is too distant (Shoshone's puppet city-state) to launch meaningful attacks. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response if Edinburgh's navy approaches (Shoshone has 2 city-states under their control: Brandenburg and Edinburgh).
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: STRENGTHEN SIAM ALLIANCE to 15/15 from 10/10. Siam remains France's only reliable vassal (5 World Congress delegates, voluntary submission). Increased positive relationship ensures Siam's continued support against England shared war and Poland secondary threat. Siam provides Lampang base for operations and valuable flank position. France needs maximum diplomatic capital during England war and World Congress voting (Decolonization threat in 7 turns). Stronger Siam bond demonstrates commitment to Allies, encouraging other civ consideration while France achieves England capitulation. Resources shift from Poland to England war - Siam becomes more critical strategic partner.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Onondaga (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Onondaga declared war on France this turn, alongside Sokoto. Though small military threat, they are England-aligned ally in England-France war. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response and signals solidarity with English opposition. With increased defenses (Antiair 90, Air 95), France can handle these declarations without disrupting primary England/Shoshone strategy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Sokoto (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Sokoto declared war on France this turn along with Onondaga. Both are England-aligned city-state allies joining England's side in England-France war. Moderate hostility (-25/-25) signals France's opposition to England-aligned coalition without extreme measures. Sokoto and Onondaga are secondary threats; primary focus remains England nuclear threat and Shoshone cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE hostility vs Poland to -25/-25 from -15/-15. Poland's naval aggression around Lhasa continues - Battleship/Dreadnought fleet threatening allied city-state. Poland has nuclear capability (5 Uranium, 13,891 military) and declared war despite previous alliance. Lhasa under naval bombardment with 5 Polish Battleships threatening our ally. Heightening hostility directs AI to prioritize Poland as secondary conquest target after Shoshone cultural elimination. Not full escalation yet but clear warning that Poland's aggression has consequences.
        - UpdatedTurn: 417
        
        ### England
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: INCREASE ENGLAND HOSTILITY to maximum (-50/-50) during capitulation phase. England at 52% war score with 100% war weariness; they also possess nuclear capability. Accelerating capitulation removes England's 19 UN votes (critical if Shoshone cultural victory fails), eliminates nuclear threat, and allows full focus on Shoshone. Oxford almost razed (1 population), London heavily damaged. No compromise - demand complete surrender. England's warmongering threat against France must end permanently.
        - UpdatedTurn: 419
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE HOSTILITY vs Shoshone to -25/-25 from -20/-20 in response to Moson Kahni producing Atomic Bomb this turn. Shoshone now has nuclear weapons capability alongside their cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced). France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers this turn, showing significant naval threat. Combined cultural AND nuclear threat requires elevated diplomatic hostility to signal France will respond decisively. Shoshone's 3621 tourism and new nuclear arsenal make them an existential threat requiring maximum military pressure.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: CRITICAL ADJUSTMENT: Shoshone produced Atomic Bomb this turn - Nuke stockpiling priority. Naval increased to 95 (from 90) - France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers, naval superiority must be reestablished. Antiair 95 (from 90) - Shoshone has Heavy Bombers, air defense critical. UseNuke remains 95 - Shoshone nuclear escalation may require preemptive or retaliatory nuclear strikes. MilitaryTraining 95 (from 90) - continuous unit upgrades essential. Offense 95 / Mobile 100 maintained - aggressive ground campaigns against Shoshone wonder cities needed. Defense reduced to 70 (from 75) - shift resources to offense. Espionage 90 - Poland spy detected in巴黎, Brazil spy in Orleans, Shoshone nuclear production requires intelligence.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - French Sphere of Influence: Venezia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Sanction: The Shoshone:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Melbourne:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Travel Ban:
            - Description: Restricts international travel between Civilizations. Reduces the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 35%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Palatium:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Bornu:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Sydney:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Brandenburg:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: England
          - Enact: French Sphere of Influence: Ecbatana:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: France
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: The Shoshone

    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.

      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):

        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):

      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):

      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):

          - type: reasoning
          - text:  We need to decide on macro-level strategy at Turn 421.
            
            Let's digest the state.
            
            We are France (Player 4), Napoleon, playing on Vox Populi mod. We are in the Information Era (tech 68, researching Stealth, next turn). We have 9 cities (Paris, Orleans, Lyon, Troyes, Edirne, Istanbul, Sukhothai, Si Satchanalai, Bursa). Several of the latter are occupied (Edirne, Istanbul, Sukhothai, Si Satchanalai, Bursa). We have conquered some capitals: Istanbul (Ottomans), Sukhothai (Siam), Paris (ours). We are also at war with basically everyone: The Shoshone, England, Brazil, Poland. Our relationship with Siam is friendly (they are our voluntary vassal). We have major city-state allies: Bornu, Hanoi, Ecbatana, Venezia, Lhasa, Palatium, Sydney, Melbourne. England has Edinburgh and Utica. Poland has Onondaga, Sokoto. Shoshone has Brandenburg, Dublin. 
            
            Victory conditions:
            - Domination: We control 3 original capitals (37%). Shoshone also 2 (25%), Poland 2 (25%). Need 8 capitals total? Actually CapitalsNeeded: 8. There are 8 major civs originally? Let's see: The Shoshone, Brazil, Siam, France, England, Poland, The Ottomans (defeated), The Aztecs (defeated). So original capitals: Moson Kahni, Rio de Janeiro, Lampang (Siam's original?), Paris, London, Warsaw, Istanbul, Tenochtitlan? Wait, original capitals list includes Tenochtitlan (Aztec). Poland controls Tenochtitlan and Warsaw. Shoshone controls Moson Kahni and Rio de Janeiro (since Brazil is vassal? Wait Brazil still exists with Rio de Janeiro; but Domination counts capitals controlled or vassalized. "CapitalsControlled" list for Shoshone includes Moson Kahni and Rio de Janeiro. That means Brazil might be a vassal? Actually Brazil is shown as "Our Vassal (Capitulated)" under Shoshone relationships but also still a major player. In Vox Populi, capitulated vassals still exist but their master gets some benefits. However Domination victory might treat vassals as controlled? Anyway.
            
            The score: France 4783, Shoshone 4784 (almost equal). We're near the endgame (turn 421 of 500). Max turns 500. Many wars ongoing. We are in strong military position (military strength 62974). Shoshone 61029, Poland 36788, England 2344 (almost dead). Brazil 1569 (dead). Siam 151 (pathetic). 
            
            Our treasury is low (88 gold, -57 GPT). But we have massive military. We're producing units. Our main rival is The Shoshone (score 4784) and Poland (3892). England is nearly dead (score 1991). Our war with England seems favorable: war score 52%, their war weariness 100% (max). We could likely force a peace or conquer them. They're close. Our cities near them: Troyes, etc. Actually we have Warwick as a neighbor? Wait Warwick is an English city. We have a tactical zone near Warwick with enemy strength 2036 vs friendly 1268, posture exploit flanks. Could try to capture more English cities.
            
            Shoshone war: War score -6% (slightly negative), our war weariness 18%, theirs 22%. They have many cities, strong tech (73). They are in Information Era too. They have completed Apollo (space ship). They could be going for Science Victory! Indeed ScienceVictory section shows ApolloComplete: 1 for The Shoshone, meaning they've built the Apollo Program. So they're the only ones with Apollo, and thus a serious science victory threat. We need to stop them or win first. We have 0 parts. Could potentially pivot to science? But given wars, maybe Domination is closer. We need 5 more capitals. But conquest in 80 turns against strong Shoshone and Poland is tough. But we have strong military and could maybe take London, Warsaw, Rio? But Brazil is far. Poland has many cities and is strong.
            
            Cultural victory: The Shoshone are considered "Contender" and have Influenced 4 civs (they need 5). They are Dominant over Brazil, Siam, Poland. They are Popular over France (51%), Rising Slowly (108% ratio). We are Influential over England, Siam, Brazil, Poland (4). We need 5 total. The Shoshone is the only one not yet Influential for us. Since they are the main competitor and we are at war, increasing tourism vs them is hard because we are at war and closed borders. Travel Ban resolution reduces tourism by 35% globally. Tourism from war is difficult. But we have huge culture per turn (2386) and tourism 1478. Shoshone tourism 3620! They are way ahead in tourism. They will likely win Cultural soon (they need 1 more civ to influential, likely us). They are Popular over us (51%, rising slowly but 108 ratio). Actually ratio 108% means they need more tourism to overcome our culture? Wait ratio of their tourism to our culture? Or our tourism to their culture? From Shoshone perspective, France: Popular (51%) - Rising Slowly (tourism/culture ratio 108%). This likely means their tourism output vs our culture? The UI shows "The Shoshone: Popular (51%) - Rising Slowly (108%)" under our influence? Wait let's interpret. Under France's influences:
            - The Shoshone: Familiar (27%) - Rising Slowly (tourism/culture ratio 36%).
            This is our tourism influence on them. We have 27% influence on them. They have 51% influence on us. So they are closer to influencing us than we are to influencing them. Cultural victory for us seems unlikely because Shoshone is ahead and we need to influence them; war reduces tourism effectiveness.
            
            Diplomatic Victory: votes needed 44. We have 33 delegates (75%). Shoshone 24 (54%). England 19 (43%). World Congress voting in 4 turns. We could potentially win Diplomatic if we get enough delegates. Decolonization proposal by England could reset city-state influence, which would hurt. Our sphere of influence resolutions have given us permanent allies with many city-states. That gives stable delegates. If we can get to 44 votes, we win. Currently 33. Need 11 more. There are some city-states we could ally with (e.g., Edinburgh, Utica, Brandenburg, Dublin, Onondaga, Sokoto). But many are hostile due to wars. Decolonization could reset influence to 50 unless permanent ally. Our spheres make some permanent allies. But not all. We have spheres: Venezia, Melbourne, Bornu, Palatium, Hanoi, Sydney, Ecbatana. That's 7 permanent allies. Others are allied but not permanent. If Decolonization passes, those non-permanent allies drop to 50. That would be a huge blow. We should try to defeat that proposal. Since it's being voted on in 4 turns, we need to consider our vote. We have 33 votes, likely enough to defeat it if we vote against. But other players may support it.
            
            Our current GrandStrategy is Conquest. Flavors heavily military: Offense 95, Defense 70, Mobilization 100, CityDefense 75, MilitaryTraining 95, Ranged 100, Mobile 100, Nuke 100, UseNuke 95, Naval 95, Air 100, Production 100, Gold 85, Science 95, etc. Expansion 0, Spaceship 10.
            
            Our research next is Electronics (set). Current research Stealth finishes in 1 turn. Electronics unlocks Police Station (security) and leads to Alternative Energy/Telecommunications. Not sure if we want to continue to science or something else. But given Shoshone is building spaceship, maybe we need to rush spaceship? No, we have no Apollo. Or we can try to capture their capital to stop them and potentially claim their cities. Since they have many cities on another continent presumably. We are "Far" from Shoshone. Distance Far. So capturing their cities is logistically difficult. But we have a strong navy. They have a massive navy too (Fleet Destroyers, Missile Cruisers). We're fighting naval battles.
            
            Given current state: At turn 421/500. Wars with Shoshone, England, Poland, Brazil. Our best path to victory:
            - Domination: Need 5 more original capitals. We could capture London (original capital). We could try to capture Warsaw (original capital). We could try to capture Rio (original capital of Brazil), but Brazil is far. We could try to capture Tenochtitlan (original capital of Aztecs, currently held by Poland). To win domination, we likely need to take Warsaw, London, Tenochtitlan, Moson Kahni, and either Rio or Lampang (Siam). But Siam is our vassal; we already control their capital? Actually we captured Sukhothai (Siam's capital). Sukhothai is listed under our CapitalsControlled. So we have Sukhothai. Wait original capitals: Istanbul, Sukhothai, Paris. Need 5 more. Original capitals remaining: London, Warsaw, Moson Kahni, Rio de Janeiro, Tenochtitlan, and maybe Lampang? Actually Lampang might be Siam's original capital, but Sukhothai is also a city. The report says Sukhothai is the capital we control. That suggests Sukhothai is the original capital. Okay. Then remaining original capitals: Moson Kahni (Shoshone), Rio (Brazil), London (England), Warsaw (Poland), Tenochtitlan (Aztecs). That's 5. If we capture all 5, we win. That's extremely ambitious in 80 turns, especially Moson Kahni is far, huge, and defended. However, our military is strong. But the AI is strong.
            
            - Science: Shoshone has Apollo, we don't. To win science, we need to build Apollo then parts. Probably not enough time.
            
            - Cultural: We need to become influential over Shoshone. War makes it hard; Travel Ban reduces tourism. Shoshone is also close to influential over us. Not likely.
            
            - Diplomatic: We need 44 votes; we have 33. Could be achievable if we ally with more city-states and/or get World Religion/World Ideology bonuses. Currently World Ideology: Autocracy (we are Autocracy, good). We get +1 delegate for each other civ following Autocracy. But only we are Autocracy? Let's see: Shoshone is Freedom, Brazil Freedom, England Order, Poland Freedom, Siam? Not sure. So only us. We do have many city-state allies. Decolonization is a threat.
            
            - Time: Score is tied with Shoshone. We need to keep lead in score.
            
            Given these, what should we do?
            
            We need to decide on:
            1. Persona adjustments (if any).
            2. Relationships with major civs (if any changes).
            3. Flavors / Grand Strategy (must set or keep).
            4. Next research (if change).
            5. Next policy (if set).
            
            Currently next research is Electronics; next policy is None.
            
            Let's consider next policy. Our policy branches: We have Autocracy (10 policies adopted: Elite Forces, MIC, United Front, Lebensraum, Air Supremacy, Lightning Warfare, Mare Nostrum, Co-Prosperity Sphere, Martial Spirit). Wait that's 9? Let's count: Elite Forces (1), MIC (2), United Front (3), Lebensraum (4), Air Supremacy (5), Lightning Warfare (6), Mare Nostrum (7), Co-Prosperity Sphere (8), Martial Spirit (9). We have not yet taken the Tier 1 tenets Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist. We are in Autocracy. The available tenets include Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist. Since we have a lot of culture and tourism, Futurism could help tourism (+2 Culture from Great Works, earn 50 Tourism when conquering a city). New World Order gives -2 Unhappiness from Distress and faster/cheaper Police Stations and Constabularies with culture/production. Iron Fist gives vassal benefits and worker improvement rate. We have a voluntary vassal (Siam). Iron Fist would give 25% increased yields from city-states and vassals, and +50% worker improvement rate, and prevents liberation of vassals. Could be decent.
            
            But we also have other branches partially filled: Imperialism (only Imperialism), Industry (1), Statecraft (2), Tradition (2), Rationalism (3), Progress (1), Authority (3), Fealty (1), Artistry (6). Next policy in 22 turns. Our culture per turn is 2386, so soon.
            
            We could also continue Autocracy and pick one of the Level 1 tenets. But policy selection should be based on our goals. If we want Domination or Diplomatic, what is best?
            
            New World Order: -2 Unhappiness and Distress in all cities, Police Stations and Constabularies give +3 Culture and +5 Production, built 100% faster. This is strong for happiness and production. Given we have many occupied cities with unhappiness (Edirne happiness delta -11, Sukhothai -12, Bursa -12), reducing unhappiness would help. Police Stations also increase security. This seems a solid choice.
            
            Iron Fist: yields from city-states +25%, vassals tribute +25%, +50% worker improvement rate. We have many city-state allies and a vassal. Could boost economy. But war weariness and occupied unhappiness is a bigger issue.
            
            Futurism: tourism from conquest and Great Works. Not crucial.
            
            Alternatively, we could adopt a policy from another branch.
